LDP 21

This surface has a descendant with elliptic boundary, described in Example B.11 loc. cit.

LDP 22–23

These surfaces have tigers, and are described in Theorem 6.2(2)–(4) loc. cit, as follows. Let
(S,C)
be a log canonical log surface such that
S
is a del Pezzo surface of rank one, and
C
is reduced, and
K_{S}+C
is anti-nef. They are classified in Section 5 loc. cit. The corresponding surface from LDP 22 is simply
S
, see Theorem 6.2(4) loc. cit. For LDP 23, let
(\widetilde{S},\widetilde{C}+D_{\widetilde{S}})
be the minimal log resolution of
(S,C)
, where
\widetilde{C}
is the proper transform of
C
; let
\pi\colon X\longrightarrow\widetilde{S}
be a sequence of blowups over some point
p\in\widetilde{C}
, and define
D
as
\pi^{*}(\widetilde{C}+D_{\widetilde{S}})
minus the
(-1)
-curve. Contracting
D
, we get the corresponding del Pezzo surface
\overline{X}
from LDP 23, see Theorem 6.2(3) loc. cit.
The initial pair
(S,C)
is as in one of the following results of §5 loc. cit:

5.1(1), 5.2(1)–(6), 5.3–5.5, 5.6(1),(3), 5.7(1),(3),(4), 5.9(2)–(4),(7),(8), 5.11(2),(3),(5)–(7),(9),(10)

Here by construction we have
0pt(\overline{X})\leqslant 0pt(S,C)\leqslant 2
, or
S=\mathbb{P}^{2}
and some pencil of lines pulls back to a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration of height at most
2
on
(X,D)
.

5.2(7), 5.6(2), 5.7(2), 5.9(1)

We claim that
0pt(S,C)\leqslant 2
, so
0pt(\overline{X})\leqslant 2
as above.

In these cases there is a birational morphism
\upsilon\colon(\widetilde{S},\widetilde{C}+D_{\widetilde{S}})\longrightarrow(Y,D_{Y})
contracting
\widetilde{C}
, such that
D_{Y}=E_{Y}+T_{0}+T_{1}+T_{2}
, where
T_{j}
are zero or disjoint admissible chains,
E_{Y}
is a smooth rational curve meeting each nonzero
T_{j}
once; and either
T_{0}=0
or
T_{1},T_{2}=[2]
. Moreover,
\upsilon^{-1}
has exactly one base point, say
p
, such that
p\in E_{Y}
; either
p\in T_{j}
or
\beta_{D_{Y}}(E_{Y})\leqslant 1
, and if
T_{0}\neq 0
then
p\in T_{0}
. Since
\widetilde{C}+D_{\widetilde{S}}
is not negative definite, neither is
D_{Y}
, which implies that
E_{Y}^{2}\geqslant-1
. Since all components of
\widetilde{C}+D_{\widetilde{S}}
have self intersection number at most
-1
, there is a decomposition
\upsilon=\vartheta\circ\vartheta_{+}
such that either
\vartheta^{-1}_{*}E_{Y}=[0]
, or
\vartheta^{-1}_{*}(T_{1}+E_{Y}+T_{2})=[2,1,2]
supports a fiber of a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration whose pullback has height at most two, as needed.

5.1(2), 5.10, 5.11(1),(4),(8)

In these cases, by construction
\overline{X}
has a descendant with elliptic boundary.

5.9(5)

We claim that
\overline{X}
has a descendant with elliptic boundary, or
0pt(\overline{X})\leqslant 0pt(S,C)\leqslant 2
as above.

By construction, there is a morphism
(\widetilde{S},\widetilde{C}+D_{\widetilde{S}})\longrightarrow(Y,D_{Y})
contracting
\widetilde{C}
such that
(Y,D_{Y})\longrightarrow(S,E)
is the minimal log resolution of a log canonical log surface such that
K_{S}+E\equiv 0
and
E
has exactly one singular point, which is a node. Lemma A.19(2) loc. cit. implies that the reduced total transform of
E
, call it
R
, is a rational circular connected component of
D_{Y}
. Let
\vartheta\colon(Y,D_{Y})\longrightarrow(Z,D_{Z})
be the contraction of
D_{Y}-R
and all
(-1)
-curves in
R
and its images. If
D_{Z}
is irreducible then
(Z,D_{Z})
is a descendant of
\overline{X}
with elliptic boundary. Otherwise,
D_{Z}
is a rational circular divisor in
Z^{\mathrm{reg}}
with a component
G
satisfying
G^{2}\geqslant 0
. As before, using the fact that all components of
\widetilde{C}+D_{\widetilde{S}}
have negative self-intersection numbers, we conclude that the total transform of
G
contains a support of a fiber of a
\mathbb{P}^{1}
-fibration of height
2
, as needed.

5.9(6)

Here
(S,C)
is as in Lemma 5.8 loc. cit. In cases 5.8(2),(3), we have by construction
0pt(S,C)\leqslant 2
. In the remaining case 5.8(1), there is a birational morphism
(\widetilde{S},\widetilde{C})\longrightarrow(S,A+B)
, where
S
is a del Pezzo surface of rank one, and
A+B
are two smooth rational curves meeting normally at two points; contained in the smooth locus of
S
(see the proof of 5.8 loc. cit). Arguing as in case 5.2(7) above we see that
0pt(\overline{X})\leqslant 2
.

LDP 24

By construction, surfaces in this class are of height at most two.

Tables

We now list all singularity types of surfaces in Theorem
A
, that is, del Pezzo surfaces of rank one and height
3
, which have no descendants with elliptic boundary. Each such singularity type is of the form
T_{1}+\dots+T_{k}
, where
T_{i}
is a type of an admissible chain or an admissible fork, which is a minimal resolution graph of the corresponding singular point. We use conventions summarized in Section
2G
. Thus
[a_{1},\dots,a_{k}]
is a chain whose subsequent components have self-intersection numbers
-a_{1},\dots,-a_{n}
; and
\langle b;T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}\rangle
is a fork with a branching component
[b]
and twigs
T_{1}
,
T_{2}
,
T_{3}
. For an integer
k\geqslant 0
we write
(2)_{k}
for a sequence consisting of the integer
2
repeated
k
times, which corresponds to a chain of
